# Changes — the index of change records

Every change in this repository has ONE dated note of its own in `agent-wiki/` — a Zettelkasten
entry that states what was verified against the spec, the decisions taken, and the issue and PR
it implements. This file is the index of those notes, newest first, and it is the file
`README.md` points at instead of carrying change history itself.

The index rows do not live here: each one is its own file, `docs/changes.d/<note-stem>.md`,
holding a single row (issue #934). A change adds its note and its fragment, and nothing else.
One row per file means two changes can never edit the same line, so no merge can re-dirty an
open PR. This page is rendered from those fragments by `make changes-index`; do not edit it by
hand.

`make check-docs` fails when a note under `agent-wiki/` has no fragment, when a fragment names
a note that does not exist, or when a row is malformed.

New tests prove a transient read is retried and pushed, a persistent read is refused, and a fork-fetch fault is a named skip. | [2026-11-07-rebase-bot-fork-head-robustness-1952.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-07-rebase-bot-fork-head-robustness-1952.md) | #1952 |

| 2026-11-07 | `engine-build` binary flag probe learns a version-only policy (issue #1888): tensorfold-cuda's `-h`/`--help`/`-V` answer nothing (exit 2) because at the 1.2.1 pin TensorFold's help is a separate subcommand, not flags — measured in the default sweep (run `42280478755`, job `128107985154`). A new `binary_probe: version` policy mirrors the existing `help` policy: the version family must answer (the binary self-proves — and the image's own `detect` re-answers `--version`), while the help family is exempt because the binary truthfully has none. `scripts/engine_image.py::decide()` skips the help-family failure for `policy == "version"`; `scripts/engine_skills.py` accepts the new policy; `skills/engines/tensorfold/SKILL.md` declares `binary_probe: {cuda: version}`; `containers/engines/binaries.json` regenerated. Not a gate weakening — `flags`/default still requires help, and a `version` cell whose version answers nothing still reds (#625 stays true). Tests: a version-only pass case and a dead-version red case in `tests/test_engine_binary_flags.py` | [2026-11-07-engine-binary-probe-version-policy.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-07-engine-binary-probe-version-policy.md) | #1888 |

| 2026-11-07 | ci(verdict): refuse a `completed` job row whose `conclusion` has not landed — `require_settled` (merged in #2017 for the `status: in_progress` half) checked only `status`, so a run still settling through the conclusion field (`status: completed`, `conclusion: null`) passed the gate, was counted `pending` and posted a false RED verdict (`conclusion=failure` check-run) for a run that was not done (#2046/#2035); a row is now settled only when BOTH `status == completed` AND `conclusion` is non-empty — the conclusion-less row is refused like an `in_progress` row (exit 2, no check-run), a genuine red and a settled `none` are untouched | [2026-11-07-ci-verdict-refuses-conclusion-less-row-2046.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-07-ci-verdict-refuses-conclusion-less-row-2046.md) | #2046 |

| 2026-11-07 | ci(verdict): add a `workflow_dispatch` manual re-judge path — `ci-verdict.yml` had no dispatch trigger, so re-judging a red verdict meant re-running the whole heavy CI fan-out, and a run whose job records had expired could never be re-judged green (`total_count` shrinks; the judge posts `none` "job(s) ABSENT"); a dispatch with `run_id` (+ optional `head_sha`) now runs the same `judge` steps read-only + check-run post (#1886) | [2026-11-07-ci-verdict-dispatch-rejudge-1886.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-07-ci-verdict-dispatch-rejudge-1886.md) | #1886 |
| 2026-11-07 | `check-pr-base` renders a stale-but-clean PR (merge-base IS a `main` ancestor, every `base..rev` carry the branch's own) with a distinct non-#845 "rebase onto a fresh origin/main" verdict and exits 0, instead of falsely accusing it of the #845 foreign-commit leak and cancelling the walking legs (issue #2016, reviewer audit b7d3edcf; measured #1976/#1969/#1933). The discriminator is ref-reachability (`scripts/check_pr_base.py::_own_vs_foreign`, `git for-each-ref --contains`, one call per commit): an own commit is reachable only from the head's own lineage, while a genuine #845 leak's inherited commit is reachable from ANOTHER ref; merge-base off `main` entirely is always foreign. A stale verdict exits 0, so ci.yml's `guard` job (which now fails only on a genuine leak) no longer cancels `linux`/`ci-images`/`scanner-images` (all `needs: [guard]`) — a unit verdict exists at the head even when the base is stale; the #845 leak still reds with the original message and the clean one-ahead negative control is unchanged. The reviewer skill gains the stale-vs-foreign routing rule (criterion 3): a stale-but-clean `check-pr-base`-only red is "code green, base stale, rebase owed", never a stand-down | [2026-11-07-ci-check-pr-base-stale-clean-attribution.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-07-ci-check-pr-base-stale-clean-attribution.md) | #2016 |
| 2026-11-07 | `check-pr-base` is now event-gated in `ci.yml` (`if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'` on the `make check-pr-base` step, issue #1867): on push-to-`main` the pushed commit IS main's history the instant the run starts, but the step re-fetched `origin/main` live and a busy fleet had already advanced it, so `merge-base(origin/main, HEAD)==HEAD != origin/main` and the gate red on a phantom base-staleness (foreign commits (0), content IS on main; measured `42209323920` @ `d7f5b33329` and `42209857316` @ `7b2c9e5d57`). The #845 protection exists to catch a PR branch cut from another PR, which a push event cannot be — the step is skipped on push and still runs the live-fetch merge-base-equals-base test unchanged on `pull_request` (`scripts/check_pr_base.py` untouched); the workflow comment now states the real event scope. Guard test RED-on-old/GREEN-on-fix; #845 foreign + clean one-ahead controls unchanged | [2026-11-07-ci-check-pr-base-pull-request-1867.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-07-ci-check-pr-base-pull-request-1867.md) | #1867 |
| 2026-11-07 | `check-pr-base` now PASSES a head that is an ancestor of the live base (`base..rev` empty, issue #1867/#1906..#1909): `scripts/check_pr_base.py::check` reds only when the head CARRIES a foreign diff (a PR branch cut from another PR always has non-empty `base..rev`), and a clean one-commit-ahead branch still passes. The #1867 event-scope guard (PR #1874) covered only the ci.yml guard-job step, but `linux/unit` runs `check-pr-base` unconditionally via `make ci-unit` — so push-to-main runs still reded on a phantom base-staleness (measured `42330525270` @ `c3dc135f` (#1804), `42330618401` @ `9679157e` (#1896), foreign commits (0)). The ancestor exemption fixes EVERY caller because it changes the gate's own semantics; the ci.yml `if:` guard stays as belt-and-suspenders. Guard tests RED-on-old/GREEN-on-fix; #845 foreign + clean one-ahead controls unchanged | [2026-11-07-ci-check-pr-base-ancestor-push-exempt.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-07-ci-check-pr-base-ancestor-push-exempt.md) | #1906 #1907 #1908 #1909 #1867 |

| 2026-11-05 | A merged PR must close an issue whose own acceptance criteria are checked (issue #1792): new `scripts/check_pr_acceptance.py` — the acceptance-side sibling of `check-pr-closing` — runs at merge time with the LIVE PR body (gh api, issue #1589) and the LIVE linked issue, reusing the gate surfaces CI already mounts (`check_pr_closing.closing_references` for the linked-issue set, `issue_body.section_body` + `acceptance_writeback.CHECKBOX_RE` for the `## Acceptance criteria` checkbox parse, imported never restated). `make check-pr-acceptance` reds a merged PR that closes an issue whose criterion is still `- [ ]` (unless named out of scope under `## Not covered` with a linked work item) or whose own `- [x]` names a criterion the issue has unchecked; wiring in `ci.yml` + `scope.yml` beside `check-pr-closing`. Verification only — the write-back stays #1644's verdict-driven seam (#1644). Previously `closes #<n>` proved only that the PR named the issue, so a #491-class auto-merged PR could close a 5/5-unticked issue and verify green | [2026-11-05-pr-acceptance-gate.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-05-pr-acceptance-gate.md) | #1792 |
| 2026-11-05 | The owner's universal form rule for PR bodies (issue #1796): a BOT PR must carry its OWN `## Acceptance criteria` `- [ ]` checklist. New `make check-pr-acceptance-form` (a `check_pr_hygiene.py acceptance-form` subcommand) refuses a bot-authored PR whose body has no (or an empty) tickable acceptance section — verification/surface only, never a #1088 merge-blocker, never a tick (that stays #1644). It reuses `acceptance_writeback.acceptance_items` (imported, never restated), is gated by `is_bot_pr` (human-only ranges skipped), and runs WITH the LIVE PR body (gh api, issue #1589) in `ci.yml` + `scope.yml` BEFORE `check-pr-acceptance` — the ordering is deliberate: the #1792 issue-state gate reds the introducing PR's own unticked issue boxes, so running the body-form gate first keeps its wire verdict from being masked by that self-red (the #1792 gate is step-order-independent and unweakened; it still runs and still holds). Profile synced byte-identical (md5=YES) | [2026-11-05-planner-guard-stale-loop-vehicles.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-05-planner-guard-stale-loop-vehicles.md) | #1689 |

| 2026-11-05 | `make bot-preflight` now surfaces the `AGENTS:`/`BASE:` body obligation before a lane pushes (issue #1790): `scripts/bot_preflight.py` reuses the CI gate's own `check_pr_hygiene.pr_agents_line_findings` (imported, never restated) over the would-be range and a prospective body — `make bot-preflight [BODY=/tmp/body.md]` refuses (exit 1) when a supplied body is missing/mis-citing the `AGENTS: <sha> <bytes>b <lines>l` + `BASE: <sha> (== origin/main at open)` pair (the #845 `BASE: == AGENTS:` equality enforced by the gate at `check_pr_hygiene.py:564`), warns loudly (exit 0) when no body is supplied yet on a bot branch, and stays silent for human-only ranges (mirroring the gate's `is_bot_pr` gating). Every verdict names issues #792/#845 and the contract location `scripts/check_pr_hygiene.py:518`. Previously the only producer of the pair requirement was the CI gate reading the LIVE PR body — unreachable from a hermetic preflight — so #1788 opened preflight-green with zero hygiene lines yet red-CI on `check-pr-agents-line` | [2026-11-05-bot-preflight-agents-base.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-05-bot-preflight-agents-base.md) | #1790 |
| 2026-11-05 | acceptance_writeback now matches the `## Acceptance criteria` heading leniently (a suffixed heading like `## Acceptance criteria (what closing means)` still ticks its boxes) and, when no acceptance section exists, raises a BodyError naming the headings actually present (canary) instead of a generic "no section" | [2026-11-05-acceptance-writeback-lenient-heading-1802.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-05-acceptance-writeback-lenient-heading-1802.md) | #1802 |
| 2026-11-05 | a review verdict now ticks the issue's acceptance boxes (issue #1644): a merged-and-reviewed issue's `## Acceptance criteria` is written back by `scripts/acceptance_writeback.py` — on a reviewed-green merge the lane holding the COMMENTED verdict patches `issues/{n}` so each `- [ ]` item the verdict proved becomes `- [x]` carrying `(proven: reviewer/review/head-sha/run-id)`, while every item the verdict did NOT prove stays unticked and is named (a trailing `- unproven: <n>. <text>` bullet); an auto-merged PR with no reviewer verdict (#1088-class) ticks nothing. Previously the boxes were decoration by construction: `scripts/auto_merge.py` had zero acceptance write-back and no PATCH `issues/{n}` existed, so issue #1638 (closed by PR #1640 with all five criteria proven by the reviewer's COMMENTED verdict `5746530389`) still read 5/5 unticked. `tick_acceptance` is pure stdlib (proved by `tests/test_acceptance_writeback.py` — ticked where proven, unproven named, empty-verdict refused, idempotent) and renders through `issue_body`, so the shape stays single-sourced (issue #745); the body PATCH is the only write that touches issue text | [2026-11-05-acceptance-writeback-1644.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-05-acceptance-writeback-1644.md) | #1644 |
| 2026-11-05 | planner lane's universal acceptance-criteria form rule now versioned in-tree (bold-heading consequence from #1792) as acceptance-form-rule.md, byte-identical to live | [2026-11-05-acceptance-form-rule-1798.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-05-acceptance-form-rule-1798.md) | #1798 |
| 2026-11-04 | `scripts/check_zero_job_pending.py` stops filing "no runner matched the labels" for engine-build runs queued behind their own concurrency group: `zero_job_pending_runs` collects the (workflow, branch) pairs of `in_progress` runs (the concurrency holders) and skips a QUEUED candidate whose pair is held — that run is waiting its turn, not stranded; an IN_PROGRESS run with zero jobs is itself the stalled one and stays, so the #1250 no-runner door is unchanged for real stranding. The `--json` run-list field set gains `status` for the classifier. A STRANDED run (group free, zero jobs) is now forced RED via the `ci-verdict` check-run path (`ci_scope.check_run_payload(RED, head, …)` → `conclusion: failure`), so a truly-unrunnable dispatch surfaces as a failed check instead of an eternal `pending` — the monitor workflow gains `checks: write`, and an already-filed run is neither re-filed nor re-forced RED (#917, #1704). Prove-the-guard: four tests — queued-behind-group (queued suppressed, in_progress holder flagged, never filed nor forced RED), cross-branch-holder (same-workflow run on its own branch stays flagged), stranded-run-filed-exactly-once-and-forced-RED (asserts exactly one `issue create` and exactly one `check-runs` POST with conclusion=failure), and stranded-reported-run never double-filed; import-roots ratchet held, reactor/verdict/frozen suite green. | [2026-11-04-zero-job-queued-behind-concurrency.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-04-zero-job-queued-behind-concurrency.md) | #1704 |
| 2026-11-04 | sec(agent): encode the owner-2026-11-01 empty-queue → own-domain continuous-improvement audit in `agents/llgenie-security/SOUL.md` (four domains: scan-coverage, dependency-pin validity, scan-rule-set freshness, admission-gate coverage; six-section `agent:implement` findings routed through the normal queue, never this lane's own fix pass, no deliberate weakening; clean audit recorded `re-audited at <sha>, no findings`) per #1245/#791/#1010 — closes #1773 | [2026-11-04-security-idle-audit-1773.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-04-security-idle-audit-1773.md) | #1773 |
| 2026-11-04 | New fleet standing rule (owner directive): **read the failing job's actual log before any dispatch or green/red/reviewable claim**. A check-run `conclusion` is a verdict, not a cause — claiming #1719 "one clean re-run from owner-reviewable" on a rollup while the heavy `linux/unit` row was still `in_progress` and its log was unretrievable burned a CI cycle. The rule: quote the failing step's OWN log (run id + job id + head sha) before ANY dispatch or green/red/reviewable claim; when the log is unretrievable mid-run, state exactly "error line not yet retrievable — unlocks at run completion" and never invent a cause; never pronounce a PR reviewable/mergeable while a heavy row is unread; never trust a stale attempt-1 `ci-verdict` over a fresh verdict at the CURRENT head. It is added to `agents/standing-rules.md` (the canonical fleet block) and mirrored **verbatim** into all 13 `agents/<profile>/SOUL.md`, so the lane needs no memory of the rule — the #873 fleet-block drift gate keeps the copies identical | [2026-11-04-read-the-failing-job-log-before-any-claim.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-04-read-the-failing-job-log-before-any-claim.md) | #1721 |

| 2026-11-02 | `auto-merge`'s `APPROVED` review body now carries a citable verdict: `approve()` interpolates the head sha being judged (`state.headRefOid`) and the run id deciding it (`$GITHUB_RUN_ID`) into the approval text, so a reader/auditor can re-fetch the evidence from the record alone — closing the issue-#1619 gap where a board-sweep found 5 merged PRs (#1210/#1247/#1259/#1294/#1555) whose only declaring verdict was generic auto-merge approval text with neither field; a PR whose state carries no head sha is never approved, and the #1273 sweep regexes (`\b\d{11,}\b` run, `\b[0-9a-f]{7,40}\b` sha) now match the approval body exactly as they already match the reviewer-lane verdict comments | [2026-11-02-auto-merge-citable-approval-1619.md](agent-wiki/2026-11-02-auto-merge-citable-approval-1619.md) | #1619 |

Running the newly-reached `ci-install-uninstall` in the producer exposed a latent gap: it must `mkdir -p ~/bin` before the foreign-file witness `touch ~/bin/not-ours`, since the producer leg never ran `make install` (which creates `~/bin` on the witness path) — without it the leg reds "No such file or directory" (confirm `41236321425`).
